EVAL ROTATION STUDY — real 1-minute MNQ + MGC (data/MNQ, data/MGC), 2020-06-28 → 2026-10-02, New York time
=====================================================================================================

MODEL
• Pool of N 50K evaluations, Take Profit Trader 50K rules from KeystoneFirmRules: EOD trailing drawdown $2,000 (floor = highest END-OF-DAY
  balance − $2,000, checked intraday against the open basket too; no lock in the evaluation), target +$3,000, consistency best day ≤ 50 %
  of the total profit at pass, min 3 trading days. MFFU Rapid EOD 50K (30 %, 4 days, $900 day cap) re-run on the best variants.
• One ROTATION = MNQ q1 + MGC q2 in the SAME direction, closed at basket ≥ +TARGET or ≤ −RISK, or 16:54 NY. Direction: random BUY-BUY /
  SELL-SELL (5 random runs added up), alternating, or with the MNQ 15-minute EMA200.
• Costs: $1.00 round trip per micro + 1 tick slippage per contract per side (MNQ $0.50, MGC $1.00).
• Round robin: after a rotation the NEXT account trades. An account stops for the day once its day profit ≥ the consistency cap
  ($1,500 TPT / $900 MFFU). Passed / blown accounts are replaced by a fresh evaluation at once (counted in "started").
• Target + consistency met with days missing → the missing days are done with tiny trades (risk-free here) and it counts as passed.
• Fee per pass = $150 × evaluations started ÷ passed (assumption: one month of a TPT 50K ≈ $150–170; resets / activation not included).
• LIMITS OF THE MODEL: the basket is checked on 1-minute CLOSES (the real software checks every tick, so real exits are closer to
  ±TARGET / ±RISK; overshoot here is symmetric); no news filter; the futures roll is whatever the data files hold.

CONCLUSIONS (plain language)
1. It is a coin flip with costs. Direction makes no difference: random 18.7 %, alternating 18.9 %, with the 15-minute trend 18.9 %
   pass rate (all settings pooled, ~2.8 million simulated evaluations). 1,028 of the 1,080 variants LOST money in total trading P/L
   across all their evaluations; the 52 positive ones are small and look like noise (their pass rates fall back with a 1–5-minute pause).
2. The pass rate is set by the rules, not by the entries: ≈ 18–27 % for every reasonable setting. A pure drift-free random walk would
   pass a +$3,000 / −$2,000 race about 40 % of the time; the EOD trailing floor, the $1,500 day cap and commissions + slippage take
   it down to ~20 %. So with $150 evaluations, ONE PASS COSTS ≈ $600–750 in fees (≈ 4–5 evaluations bought per pass).
3. What helps the pass rate (slightly): SMALL size (MNQ 2 + MGC 2 — fewer contracts = less cost per rotation), a SHORT session
   (9:30–11:30 NY best 20.6 %, all-day worst 17.8 %), a bigger pool (20 accounts 19.6 % vs 5 accounts 17.9 %), and a risk/target
   that is wide relative to the costs: 1000/1500 20.6 %, 750/1500 19.8 %, 500/1500 (yours) 19.3 %, 500/500 17.7 %, 300/900 16.6 %.
   Big size + all day + small risk (many rotations) is the worst: 12–13 %.
4. Your setting (risk $500 / target $1,500, random BUY-BUY / SELL-SELL): 20–24 % with MNQ 2 + MGC 2 in 9:30–11:30, ≈ $630–750 of
   fees per pass, 7–12 trading days to pass. It works as a "buy many tickets" plan, not as an edge: expect ~1 pass in 4–5 evaluations.
5. FUNDED (intraday trailing $2,000 that stops at the start balance, success = +$2,100 for a first payout): best ≈ 27–31 % reach the
   first payout (MNQ 2 + MGC 2, 9:30–11:30, risk 300–500). So a random rotation turns ~$700 of fees into a pass and only ~1 in 4
   passes into a first payout → roughly $2,500–3,000 of fees per first payout. Note: TPT forbids automation on funded (PRO) accounts.
6. Overfitting warning: the top rows of the table (24–27 %) are the luckiest of 1,080 variants; re-run with a 1–5-minute pause they
   fall back to 20–26 %. Use the pooled numbers above, not the best row. To beat the coin flip the ENTRY needs an edge
   (see docs/progress/sltp-study-6y.txt for the setups that did hold up on gold).
